Architect's bridge from this suite to the next phase. One entry per gap; factual; evidence is
a `blade emit`/`blade plan` excerpt, a diagnostic code, or a `src/` location found cheaply.
"Direction" is one line, not a plan. Lane column: whether the C++ and LLVM lanes differ.
Evidence dated 2026-10-17, compiler 0.20.0, this worktree's `bin/Release/net10.0/Blade.exe`;
blade-B's items cite `notes/llvm-acceptance-B.md`.

| G6 | **FIXED (agent D, merge b3272712: M1 retained-block pool cache in BOTH allocators + M3 let-rec zero-fill elision via a new `IRUninit` leaf, formalism §7.5; M0 shim build identity).** D's scratch A/B (full size, serial, 7 interleaved runs, medians; NOT harness-measured): recur C++ 136.4 → 23.8 ms, LLVM 131.5 → 22.3 ms; page faults 106,906 → 13,626 and 94,606 → 13,277; checksum identical. Still open on this kernel: 4.5b(ii) destination passing and 4.5c stage fusion (G8). Historical entry: recur (serial) — **pilot-diagnosed: the ALLOCATOR, not the arithmetic**; phase-2 plan adds two unrecorded passes (plan §1.5, §4.5): a 48 MB ZERO-FILL of the whole trajectory before the recursion (`src/TypeCheckInfer.fs:13840`) and a 48 MB COPY of each step's result into the trajectory row — fixes 4.5b(i) zero-fill elision and 4.5b(ii) destination passing, after the retained-block cache 4.5a | Scalar RK4 stages in one `do x` loop, no stage arrays | Each time step allocates and frees 8 temporaries of 160 KB (4 `rhs` results, 3 stage inputs, the step result) in both lanes; the Windows heap returns freed blocks of that size as fresh pages, so each allocation re-takes ~38 page faults at ~1.3 µs: 300 steps x 8 x 38 ≈ 91k faults ≈ 118 ms — the measured 19.6 ns per cell-step against ~2.5 ns of arithmetic (pilot: 118 ms vs the expert's 10–11 ms, an 11x loss). The stage loops themselves are fully vectorized AVX2, no bounds checks, direct row pointers, no per-cell calls; `blade plan` shows pool reuse DECLINED in `step`. SCHEDULING ADMISSION MATRIX -- every pass asks `Optimize.scheduleMayWrite` (W: the shared provenance-aware `writeAnalyzer` over the module's lets) about all five parties; `cseFactsOf` (F: MayWrite, R: Repeatable) stands beside it where it already did: hot-row RowFold = producer W+F, consumer W+R, outer kernel W+F, seed W+F, between W+R; cell epilogue (ChunkFold) = producer W, consumer W (+ the Identity/Square cell-term whitelist), outer W, seed W, between W+R; compact row classes = producer W+F, consumer (walk) W+F, outer W+F, seed W+F, between W+F; row-fold fusion = producer (the recursion, its own buffer local) W + bufferUseOk/callsRepeatable, consumer W + tilePureBody, outer W + tilePureBody, seed W, between (the seed statements, inside the producer block) W + bufferUseOk/callsRepeatable; compaction fusion = producer the stored compound source, consumer the mask predicate (predOk: pure, total), outer builtin `+` only, seed none (unseeded), between W+F. The matrix covers the PRODUCER-SCHEDULING passes only (it is not a claim that every reordering pass uses one predicate): domain diagonal walks keep domainFoldOrder / domainMapKernelPure, invariant hoisting and CSE their cseFactsOf / deferred-name checks, sibling-fold sharing its own writesIn closure, elementwise / slice-write fusion tilePureBody, G35 row slots the IRTotality whitelist, G7 joins joinEffectAudit + joinThreadAudit, W2-A scatter its strict pure-body recognition over stored sources. The planners resolve callees through the PROGRAM's functions (`scheduleMayWriteIn programFuncs`), so an imported pure producer no longer declines. The backend analysis's former conservatism (CopiedLet = false in CodeGenExprSupport) is gone with G46: it now reads Lowering's rule (`isCopiedArrayLet`), since every lane copies. that shape was G46's false alias, and the case is now `omp_nest_kernel_let_mut_copy_keeps_team`); 21 the parallel-body refusal keeps the function's OWN `mut` parameters apart from captures (`loops/502` runs; the 843cb654 refusal of it was a regression); and a self-recursive function is summarized as the LEAST fixpoint from "writes nothing" (the pessimistic seed made a pure recursive helper forwarding an array look like a writer, so 843cb654 refused `examples/mandelbrot.bladenb`'s `where omp` `far_grid` -- found by a 181-file rescan; `loops/504` pins the shape); Codex review W2F-6: 22 a callback JOINS every candidate any call site passes, at each application (the resolver used to pick one candidate on a boolean: `launch(forward)` before `launch(bump)` compiled; `mutability-errors/030`, reversed order `031`, a pure candidate forwarded through recursion keeps its team `loops/505`); 23 recursion is a true fixpoint whose budget (64 rounds) widens to TOP (External plus every storage parameter) and drops the summaries built on the unfinished value (a ten-parameter rotation reaching a captured array compiled before: `032`; a callback through a recursive helper `033`); Codex review W2F-7: 24 a NULLARY function type (the empty-slot arrow `() -> T`) is a function for the callback table (the value-slot filter of W2F-6 skipped it, so a pure `launch(one)` was refused BL4005: `loops/506` runs and keeps its team, the writing twin `mutability-errors/035`); 25 the callback table is ONE collector, `Optimize.callbackTable`, shared by Lowering's parallel-body refusal and both emitters, computed to a FIXPOINT over indirect calls -- `h(f)` with `h` a callback parameter is resolved to ALL of h's candidates and propagates its actuals into each target's parameter slots (`via(launch, bump)` used to leave `bump` out of launch's set: `mutability-errors/036`, reversed order `037`, a writing lambda argument `038`; all-pure `loops/507` and pure-lambda `loops/508` keep the team) -- and a callback parameter of a function that ESCAPES untracked (referenced other than as a call head or argument, or passed to an unresolvable call) is TAINTED: no table entry, so its applications read as unknown (a write); (b) a 68-parameter rotation reaching a captured array is still climbing at round 64 and is refused through the TOP widening (`039`). Codex review W2F-8 replaced that collector's matching with a monotone abstract interpretation solved to its LEAST fixpoint (`Optimize.callbackTable`): every tracked name -- callback parameter, local `let`, MODULE binding (its identity kept) -- starts at BOTTOM and grows by joins; a call feeds every target its head may denote, known targets kept beside an unknown one; TOP comes only from an untracked name, a function a call returns, or an ESCAPE (returned, stored, used as a kernel, handed to an unknown call or a non-callback parameter); tuples are followed component-wise; a reassigned tracked name joins the new value; over budget the table widens to empty (every callback unknown). 26 a module alias `let run = launch` is an alias, not an escape (804516d1 refused the pure `loops/509`); 27 a local alias of a callback, called directly or through a tuple, is evaluated, not matched by name (804516d1 compiled the writers `mutability-errors/040`, `041`; pure controls `loops/510`, `511` keep the team); 28 an undiscovered callback is bottom, not unknown, so the answer is independent of discovery order (804516d1 refused the pure third-order `loops/512`; reversed order `513`). Deliberately conservative: the join-leg refusal and the planners' `scheduleMayWrite` / `scheduleMayWriteIn` install `Callbacks = None`, so ANY callback application there reads as a write (a declined schedule / BL4005 on a join leg, never a wrong answer). Not expressible today, so not pinned: candidates writing different `mut` parameter positions (a function type has no `mut` parameters). Own-parameter writes inside a `where omp` KERNEL are refused by the front end (the parameter is bound outside the kernel body) for both the same-cell race (`034`) and the per-cell-disjoint variant -- a conservatism; a function's own non-kernel body may write it (`loops/502`); `blade test omp-pragma` (decline needles + provenance positives), `omp-reduce` (`g41_*` runtime matrix: serial build, T = 1, 5x T = 8, total AND counter), `llvm goldens` (no fold FMF over a writing map) | both (C++: teams, K lanes, simd; LLVM: fold fast-math over a writing map -- it opens no team) | done |

CONTRACT DECIDED (formalism 10.2, new paragraph "Effectful named computations"): a `let` bound to a bare map whose evaluation WRITES (kernel, callee, or named deferred producer it reads; the shared `scheduleMayWriteIn` predicate) is evaluated ONCE, COMPLETELY, at its definition, in ascending storage order -- as `\|> compute` would -- in every scope; a pure map keeps the deferred reading. "Evaluate per read everywhere" was rejected: a function body has no downstream forcing site (S2's reason to exist), so per-read would need new codegen. Implemented as `Lowering.forceEffectfulModuleLets` (module bindings and top-level block spines, before `refuseWritingJoinLegs`) plus `TypeCheckInfer.tryInferReduceCompute`'s `namedWritingProducer` decline (the fold splice copied a NAMED producer into each fold, so forcing the let alone gave THREE evaluations, 68 / counter 12). S2's join exemption is kept in both scopes: a writing map read ONLY as reduction-join operands stays deferred and is refused (BL4005, `mutability-errors/014`, `020`; the C++ reduction-join emitter needs the leg unforced); a writing map with any other reader (alias, view, tuple, fold) is forced and a join through that reader reads stored cells -- which is what a function body already did, so five former G43 refusals are now ACCEPTANCES with one value in every lane (`mutability-errors/015, 018, 024, 025, 026` moved to `loops/519`-`523`). Deferred WRAPPERS (choice / fallback / guard / sequence) are not forced; a join over a writing one stays refused (`mutability-errors/023`). Contract (formalism 2.7, new paragraph): an assignable array `let` of an existing array holds a deep copy with the source's storage class in every scope and lane, compound / sparse / ragged / dependent-index stores excepted (the interpreter's `isDenseCopyableArray`, genVarAliasBinding's guard). `CodeGenExpr.kernelLetCopy` makes the expression-position and statement-position kernel-body lets copy (`materializeArrayCopyForm`) whenever the module records the let as assignable (`IRModule.MutableArrayLets`, now installed for the emitters as `programMutableArrayLetsCell`); the copy is freed at the IIFE's end when it provably dies there (scalar result, only full element reads / writes / extents), else left to the IIFE leak convention; it is ELIDED when unobservable (no assignment, call or callable reference in scope and a storage-free result), so a read-only `let row = X` in a kernel allocates nothing. The backend write facts now read such a let as a private copy (`CopiedLet = isCopiedArrayLet`, Lowering's rule), so a `where omp` kernel writing its copy keeps the team (`deallocArgsFor` moved to CodeGenState to free the copy) | corpus `loops/517` (write through the copy, the same under `where omp`, the copy handed to a `mut` parameter, a plain `let c = Gm` read after the kernel writes Gm, a read-only alias, a SYMMETRIC initializer; all lanes); omp-pragma `omp_nest_kernel_let_mut_copy_keeps_team` (replaces `omp_nest_write_veto_census`: pragma + `std::copy_n`, no census -- the nest veto has no other accepted shape now, every other parallel write to captured storage is BL4005 in Lowering) | C++ lane | done |

Parameters that may hold a function -- a function type or a tuple with a function leaf -- are tracked, tuples component-wise; only function-typed names are exported. Now a fold operand naming a deferred wrapper (`CodeGenFusion.isDeferredWrapperOperand`, through deferred aliases) is forced first, like a positional read: `collectDeferredPositionalReads` notes it under IRReduce (expression position: blocks, `reduce(q) + reduce(q)`), and genBindingDispatch's IRReduce arm forces it (`forceDeferredArrayInput`) before genReduceBinding. In MAIN the nodes only MARK themselves: a top-level BLOCK statement (a let chain) FORCES UP FRONT, before any block effect or alias upgrade, the C++ lane's pre-pass set in its order (fourth fix round after codex-diff-W4B-B-4: EmitLlvm.collectPositionalReadsLl, the SAME walk shape as CodeGenFusion.collectDeferredPositionalReads line for line -- IRIndex / IRExtent / IRContains bases, assignment RHSs, call arguments, every prodsum operand, a fold over a deferred choice / fallback; the slice / curry / subset children only; a chain ending in a tuple, its lets' values only, genLetChainBinding's rule -- over the lane's live deferred state, Ctx.StmtForceOrder), EVERY collected map with no exception (fifth fix round after codex-diff-W4B-B-5 finding 1: the fourth round skipped a map whose KERNEL audited total, but its deferred INPUT could fail -- `bad = map(x, 10 / v); h = map(bad, v + 1); later = map(x, x((v + 5 : Row)))`, `{ let first = h(0); first + h(1) + later(0) + later(1) }` over x = [1, 0] reached later's BL8006 at line 5 in LLVM alone; now bad's BL8013 at line 3, as in C++ and the base; producerCannotFail, still used by the partial / any-order retention, now also requires every input array to be stored or a range); any other ordinary force keeps the BASE boundary, the START of the whole statement. Fix for all three: follow aggregate carriers and projections by binder identity (a), store the first forced value with dominating lifetime or refuse the shape (b), force / copy at a local alias in every scope (c); pin each.** **FIXED (round 3 wave 4, W4-1, fix round after reviews/wave4-codex-reviews.md#codex-diff-w4-1 findings 2, 3, 6; the alias / forcing contract, formalism 10.2 "Aliases and printing of deferred maps").** `let g = method_for(x) <@> ...; let g2 = g` printed `g` (never `g2`) in the C++ lane, `g2` in the interpreter, both in the LLVM lane; the C++ lane forced `g` AT the alias | -- | CONTRACT: a top-level alias of a deferred map is the SAME deferred value: it evaluates nothing; a use through it is that use of the source (a fold fuses: the type checker resolves a TOP-LEVEL alias to a fixpoint, stopping at a block-local binder, TypeCheckInfer.blockLocalVids, by identity); a deferred map and its top-level aliases form ONE ALIAS CLASS (`IRLift.deferredAliasClasses`, shared by the three lanes' print passes) that prints under every name iff any member was forced at module level (C++ genPrintStatements declares an unforced member as a reference to a forced one; the interpreter's Run.fs reads the forced member's cells; the LLVM lane marks the class); a WRITE through a deferred binder -- element, whole reassignment, a `let` view, a writing `mut` argument (Optimize.callableWriteSummaryWith) -- is REFUSED at lowering (`Lowering.refuseDeferredWritesAndUnorderedIfs`, BL4005, by binder identity: module bindings, their aliases, bare maps in top-level blocks), so no member of a class can diverge from another and no fused fold can follow a write. A block / function-body alias is a local copy made at the alias (the C++ lane and the interpreter's IRLet arm), ordinary storage. FIX ROUND 2 (second look findings 1-3): the written-binder judgment is a PROGRAM-WIDE FIXPOINT by binder identity (`Lowering.refuseDeferredWritesAndUnorderedIfs`): the binders a write's target may belong to -- through every `let` view, alias, REBINDING and `if` of one, in any scope, stopping only at a PROVED copy (an assignable `let a = Z` of a plain dense array) or fresh storage; a walk that exhausts its budget REFUSES -- plus every argument a call passes to a parameter in the set (any number of hops), every array argument of an UNRESOLVED callee (a callback: conservative, even a reading one is refused), and the inputs of a map whose kernel writes a parameter. It replaces the first fix round's use of Optimize.callableWriteSummaryWith, whose walk stops at the first external write. The LLVM lane canonicalizes an alias class at the FORCING EVENT (materializeExpr: every slot holding the forced producer object is upgraded to the forced pool), so a read or print of any alias reads the stored cells and never reruns the producer over inputs written since; the class value is fixed at its first forcing event in every lane. FIX ROUND: (finding 2) the LLVM lane's reassignment no longer needs a forcing order -- refused; (finding 3) forcing the source now prints every alias (class rule), and writes in either direction are refused; (finding 6) the by-NAME write veto in the fused fold is gone (TypeCheck.moduleAssignedNames reverted): shadowing writers no longer change the module fold, pinned. | G199 | found by round 4 wave 6 lane C (C3 witness, pre-existing, independent of any optimizer gate): an IMPORTED deferred map forced by another module, then its input written, then read again -- `H: let mut x: Array<Float64 like Idx<3>> = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0]; let d = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> v * 2.0`; `Main: import H; let p3 = method_for(H.d) <@> lambda(v) -> v + 1.0; let q3 = method_for(p3) <@> lambda(v) -> v * v; let r3 = reduce(q3, (+), 0.0); H.x(0) = 10.0; let z = H.d(0)` -- prints `z = 2.0`, `H.d = [2.0, 4.0, 6.0]` in the C++ lane and the interpreter (the fold forces H.d persistently, a snapshot) but `z = 20.0`, `H.d = [20.0, 4.0, 6.0]` in the LLVM lane (BLADE_LLVM=1, with BLADE_COMPUTE_ELIDE=0 too: it re-evaluates the deferred map at each read) | the LLVM lane memoizes a deferred top-level map at its first force, as the other two lanes do | a lane divergence on a printed value; `blade test llvm`'s differential does not walk multifile/ so no gate sees it | round 4 wave 6 lane C | LLVM | **FIXED (round 4 wave 7, lane B, B1).** The defect was not import-specific and was a CLASS: (a) the LLVM lane recorded only the PRINTABILITY of a binding another force names (markForced) and kept its producer, so a later read and the print reran it over inputs written since; (b) a fold over a deferred computation never made the persistent forces the C++ lane makes when it evaluates a private operand (genReduceComputeBinding deferredOperandIds); (c) a producer captured each operand ArrVal when it was bound, so a dependent map read a forced operand (or a rebound array) STALE (`let z = d(0); x(0) = 10.0; let w = p(0)`: 21.0 vs 3.0), also through a deferred alias, a choice, a mask and a wholly deferred tuple; (d) a tuple-member copy, `g \|> compute`, `extents(d)` and a row view `d(0)` made no snapshot. Fix (EmitLlvm.fs, programs that assign anything, main): markForced stores a newly marked producer hoisted to the statement start (persistForced), inputs first (markForcedChildren before a force emits its producer, the C++ order); forcePrivateOperandInputs mirrors deferredOperandIds; Ctx.ProducerDeps records the operand ArrVals a producer captured and refreshProducers rebuilds a stale producer over the current slots after every force and whole-array rebind (a rebuild that would emit code refuses, C++ fallback); every statement pre-forces its positional reads (the C++ per-binding pre-pass); forceCopyInputs for copies. Witnesses: corpus loops/679-683, multifile/049-054 (054: a function-body force stays private, G75). THE GATE: `blade test llvm` now walks multifile/ (runMultiCase over lowerMultiSourceCaptured). Codex design review (round 1) listed the members above; the failure-order members it predicted (a fused private operand, a no-assignment chain) do not reproduce (both lanes report the same BL8013). Codex diff review round 2 (NO-MERGE, 3 findings, all fixed and witnessed): the rebuild re-entered itself through a row operand materialization (stack overflow; refreshProducers is now non-reentrant and runs only for a force that re-pointed a slot, loops/684); a producer rebuilt after a hoisted force broke the hoist baseline, so a later force landed inside a nest (invalid IR; rebuilt slots after a hoisted force join HoistedIds, loops/685); a kernel capture read only through `extents(d)` was never forced (markForcedChildren now forces the captures of a kernel it meets, loops/685). Codex diff review round 3 (NO-MERGE, 3 findings, all fixed and witnessed): a named loop / object_for application walked its kernel before its input, so the capture failed first (BL8013 at the wrong line; the walk now marks an apply inputs, then loop and kernel, a call its arguments before the callee, loops/686); an inline `map |> compute`, an inline tuple member and a fold over an inline map skipped the kernel-capture force (forceCopyInputs walks inline values, the fold prelude forces leaf and fold kernel captures first, the C++ order, loops/687); a whole-array rebind inside a block kept a stale hoist certificate (an inline re-point clears HoistedIds for the rebuilt slots and sets InlineRepointed: a later unhoistable force of a module map refuses, C++ fallback, loops/688). Codex diff review round 4 (NO-MERGE, 3 findings, all fixed and witnessed): an extents-only capture after an inline rebind left its snapshot unplaced (persistForced now refuses an unhoistable persistent force after an inline re-point, loops/689); the fold capture prelude stored a capture that is also a private operand (it now applies the C++ filters: not a private operand, not on the leaf spine, a deferred map; the C++ and interpreter lanes disagree on that shape themselves, G214); a Reynolds-wrapped kernel hid its captures (resolved through resolveKernel, loops/690). Codex diff review round 5 (NO-MERGE, 2 findings, both fixed and witnessed): a fold kernel capture reached through a deferred ALIAS (or a projection of a wholly deferred tuple) was not forced (the capture predicate follows them, loops/691); in a program WITHOUT assignments an extents-only capture was never evaluated, so its BL8013 disappeared (a kernel capture of a deferred module map is now forced -- stored hoisted -- in every program, forceCapture, loops/692). Codex diff review round 6 (NO-MERGE, 2 findings, both fixed and witnessed): forcing a capture of a fused consumer map before its (marked, not stored) input reversed the C++ input-then-capture failure order (a map whose kernel capture is evaluated now evaluates its deferred inputs first, loops/693); a capture through a projection of a TUPLE ALIAS (`let u = t; let b = u[0]`) was missed (the tuple parent is resolved through its aliases, loops/694). Codex diff review round 7 (NO-MERGE, 1 STRUCTURAL finding, fixed and witnessed): mandatory capture evaluation still depended on the assignment gate -- a computed `map |> compute`, a copy or a tuple member skipped it, and materializeExpr reached the captures only AFTER the producer cells (a capture failure after the producer own failure); the prerequisite walk (markForcedChildren before the producer, forceCopyInputs) now runs in every program, only the snapshot persistence of marked inputs stays gated (loops/695-696). Codex diff review round 8 (NO-MERGE, 3 findings, all fixed and witnessed): an ANONYMOUS map evaluated in place (a prodsum / mask / negation operand) built its producer with no capture force (applyToArr now forces the deferred inputs, then the deferred captures, of every map it builds while evaluating -- Ctx.BuildingDeferred marks the binding of a deferred MODULE value and a stale-producer rebuild, which evaluate nothing; loops/697); a private fold operand still gated its prerequisite walk on assignments (ungated, loops/698); a named deferred TUPLE copied as a member skipped its members (forceCopyInputs walks them, loops/699). The inline re-point refusal is narrowed to the ids a refresh REBUILT inline (a rebound kernel-local `out` keeps its inline materialization, memfree/056). Codex diff review round 9 (NO-MERGE, 3 findings, all fixed and witnessed): binding a BLOCK-LOCAL deferred map forced its captures although the block never read it (BuildingDeferred now covers every deferred-form binding; a local let forces its prerequisites where it is CONSUMED, consumePrereqs, loops/700); a capture-free anonymous map skipped its deferred INPUT captures (the input prerequisites are walked, loops/701); copying a deferred tuple forced every member capture before the first member copy (members are copied in order, each its own positional point, loops/702). The job at e6a4223a found loops/042 printing two extra bindings: inline and local walks are now CAPTURE-ONLY (markNamedInWith false: no print / snapshot marking of the inputs, which the existing paths decide). Codex diff review round 10 (NO-MERGE, 2 branch regressions, both fixed and witnessed): the deferred-form test of a binding missed `<&>` / `<&!>` tuples, so an unread parallel or fused pair forced its captures (all deferred forms, nested tuples included, loops/703); a nested named tuple copy fell back to forcing every member ahead of the first copy (the source leaves are aligned recursively with the flat parts; an unaligned tuple keeps the base order, loops/704). The corpus witnesses were renumbered loops/668-693 -> 679-704 (lane numbering collision). Codex diff review round 11: no regression against the base, verdict MERGE; the remaining PRE-EXISTING capture-evaluation members are G216. Left: G213, G214, G216 |
| G200 | found by round 4 wave 6 lane C (C4 class-closure round, the join-aggregate member's CLASS): plans that read the print selection (`BLADE_PRINT` / `--print`) by a binding's UNQUALIFIED name run per module BEFORE Lowering qualifies an imported module's bindings (`Lib.x`), so a selection naming `Lib.y` does not match `y`; join-aggregate is fixed (last-segment match, fail closed), the other readers are not audited: `Optimize.fuseSelectedTerminalModule` (`selectedNames.Contains`), `CodeGenPrivateSpool`'s print-selection gate, `planStreamGroupFold` / `planKeyedCellProjection` / `planProviderSinkStream` / `planAssemblyDest` (each requires 'a print selection excludes it', some also a single-module program) | every print-selection test resolves names to binding ids after qualification (or matches the last dotted segment, fail closed) | an imported module's printed binding may be elided (a null or undeclared print) where a plan admits multi-module programs | witness shape: `Lib: <the plan's admitted program>`; `Main: import Lib; let total = 1`; `BLADE_PRINT=Lib.<elided binding>,total` | both | **FIXED (round 4 wave 7, lane B, B2).** ONE parser and resolver, `src/PrintSelection.fs` (compiled before the optimizer): `names` (the parse; `CodeGenState.printSelection` forwards to it, so printers compare final names exactly as before), `mayName` / `mayPrint` / `excludes` (CONSERVATIVE pre-qualification matching: an entry selects a binding by its name or its last dotted segment, fail closed -- the Codex design review preferred it to an installed module-qualifier table: no lifecycle, sufficient for the contract), and `isInternal` (`CodeGen.isInternalBindingName` forwards). Every pre-qualification reader routes through it: fuseSelectedTerminalModule, compute elision (top level), transpose named chains, planStreamGroupFold, planStreamMaskReplay, planKeyedCellProjection, planProviderSinkStream, planAssemblyDest, the top-level sibling folds, OptimizeSort.planSortTerminal, OptimizeStreamGroup (stream-group-aggregate) and OptimizeJoinAgg (its `selects` is now a call). The audit (Codex design review round 1): all but stream-group-aggregate already admitted single-module programs only (their flags kept) or declined on an unknown entry; STREAM-GROUP-AGGREGATE admitted an imported `g` under `--print Lib.g` and dropped its storage (fixed; `blade test zarr` qualified-selection case). Two more members found by the review, fixed: assembly-dest treated every `__` name as unprinted BEFORE reading the selection, so `--print __y` lost `__y` (the explicit selection is read first; `blade test optimize` decision_assembly_dest_declined_selected_internal_name); a REPL / notebook evaluation supplies its own print demand AFTER lowering, so an ambient BLADE_PRINT could license a plan to drop a binding the evaluation prints (ReplSession lowers under `PrintSelection.withoutSelection` when it has its own demand). Post-qualification readers (codegen, the LLVM print pass, the interpreter) already compared final names. |

| G204 | found by round 4 wave 6 lane d (Codex class-closure review round 4 of G184): two LLVM-lane per-application leaks the G184 kernel scope does not close, each PRE-EXISTING (the arena kept them before G184 too; no unsound free was found in rounds 2-4): (1) a deferred ROW map whose row parameter is materialized inside its application -- `type R = Idx<7>; type C = Idx<1001>; function sumrow(a: Array<Float64 like C>) -> Float64 = a((0 : C)) + a((1 : C)); LLVM lane only, C++ unchanged).** (1) A kernel application OWNS a parameter bound to an UNSTORED value no other binding shares (a row view of a deferred producer): `withKernelScope` takes the applied parameters (`PoolScope.OwnedIds`) and `keepPool` treats that scope as the owner of the parameter's materialization, so the row pool is freed at the application cleanup (memfree/060; `blade test llvm` g204_row_parameter_owned_by_application -- the proof now also rejects a null store of the slot between its tracking store and the cleanup). `keepPool` only ever LENGTHENS a lifetime (it touches scopes younger than the owner; Codex design review: a reused parameter could otherwise pull an ancestor-owned pool into a younger application -- memfree/062 witnesses `peek(tmp)` after a top-level `peek(seed)`), and every SHARER upgraded by a force is kept like the forced binding. (2) VIEW PROVENANCE: `rowView` records each GEP register's base pool (`Ctx.ViewBase`); the escape test compares ROOT pools on both sides (bindings that existed before, their tuples, hoisted forces; a parameter's pool excluded) and `keepPool` keeps the ROOT of an escaping view; unknown provenance stays an escape, recorded in the IR (`; G204: kernel application pools kept -- ...`) (memfree/061). A rebinding of an outer array INSIDE a kernel applied per cell -- the G204 program itself -- emitted invalid IR (the rebound register born in the cell loop did not dominate the reads after it: clang `Instruction does not dominate all uses`, a hard BLADE_LLVM failure, never run before): `emitCountedLoopTo` now refuses by name any loop-body rebinding of a binding that existed at loop entry unless it is to a pool register a binding already held (`out = a`), a hoisted force or a callable parameter; the C++ lane takes the program (memfree/063, `g204_per_cell_rebinding_refused`). Codex: design review BUILD WITH CHANGES (2 findings: the lifetime-contraction member, provenance normalization -- both built); diff review round 1 MERGE WITH CHANGES (3: the existing-storage exemption gave a WRONG value after a zero-trip loop -- memfree/064 --, a LIVE row parameter forced in a nested loop emitted invalid IR -- memfree/065 --, a hoisted alias was falsely refused), round 2 MERGE WITH CHANGES (2: hoisted-value equality admitted a later assignment -- memfree/067 --, an array rebound in one if arm emitted invalid IR -- memfree/066), round 3 MERGE WITH CHANGES (2: the right operand of a short circuit, a choice fallback, a guard body and a match guard bypassed the guard -- memfree/068 --, and a conservative refusal: an array rebound and consumed entirely inside one if arm, valid IR before, now takes the C++ lane; liveness would recover it, NOT built). Every member is closed by ONE guard, `withStableArrays` (the counted loop body, if / match arms, the conditional operands), admitting only the exact transitions of a HOISTED force (`Ctx.HoistTransitions`) and a callable parameter not live in an enclosing application (`Ctx.LiveParams`). MEASURED (lane-d tip ca57ab45 vs base 62427ae2, LLVM lane, the G204 (1) program at R = 2003 rows of C = 50021 cells, `peak.py`: GetProcessMemoryInfo PeakWorkingSetSize after exit, 5 interleaved runs, under the lock as d-measure): peak working set 769.2 -> 4.3 MiB (median), wall 240 -> 59 ms (median, indicative), total = 4012009.0 in both. Before: **OPEN** | the two programs above (with 055's `big` folded beside (2)) | LLVM only | EmitLlvm.withKernelScope / keepPool: application-owned parameter materializations; backing-pool provenance through views |

Diff review round 2 MERGE WITH CHANGES (1: a let-bound lambda WITH a default, or an IMPORTED callee, named by the candidate -- its default elaborates at the use and reads the using scope, BL3012 -- fixed: such a candidate is not spliced, nor any in a module importing wholesale). (ii) the C++ lane binds an inline ROW VIEW operand of a fold once, before the seed, in both the statement and the expression emitter (`CodeGenExpr.InlineRowView`; loops/753, 754). Codex: design review (1) STAGE as proposed / (i) BUILD / (ii)-(iii) BUILD WITH CHANGES; diff review round 1 MERGE WITH CHANGES (2: the LLVM override declined a later capture force, the structural cache key -- fixed); round 2 MERGE WITH CHANGES (1: a total operand fused past a writing seed -- fixed); round 3 (1: the statement-start hoist of the private pool -- replaced by the refusal above); round 4 MERGE WITH CHANGES (1: a writing leaf map -- fixed, loops/772); G225, G227, G224, G223 MERGE within their scope. Remainders G243 (dependent private operand print set), G244 (a nested share). Recorded by the wave-end fable review (no change): (i) `jointTraversal` runs its effects -- gateInputs, resolveFusedArraySource of the leg / share inputs, runtime extents, capture binding -- BEFORE its extents comparison (Interp/Loops.fs ~2180-2247), and on a mismatch the per-leg path repeats them: a cost only, a join leg may not write (BL4005, G43); (ii) the C++ lane REFUSES join legs of unequal extent (CodeGenBinding.fs ~9172) while the interpreter computes them: a pre-existing asymmetry; (iii) the `InlineRowView` comment ("nothing to free") overstates: a COMPACT partial read `S(i)` renders through the compact arm and is bound by `auto` once per fold -- a leak at worst, and an improvement over the per-cell re-evaluation it replaced. Before (wave 7): **PARTLY CLOSED (round 4 wave 7, lane d; interpreter only); (1) OPEN.** (2) The sparse-range, compound-range and compound-halo arms force the kernel captures (in capture order) BEFORE resolving the keys / mask (loops/668, 670: the compiled lanes report the captured division, the interpreter used to report the key / mask modulo); (3) the dense arm runs the BL8009 halo extent guard after the inputs and the capture prelude (loops/671). Two more members found by the Codex class review, fixed: the dense arm resolves the NAMED operands, then the captures, then the INLINE operand forms (loops/673: an inline `mask(d, ..)` operand modulo vs a captured division); a fold over a deferred map evaluates its SEED after the capture prelude and the operand forcing, a join after the prelude and before the traversal (loops/674). (1) OBSERVABLE (Codex review): a join leg over a NAMED map of the shared slot -- `let e = method_for(d) <@> lambda(a) -> 100 / a; let p = method_for(e) <@> lambda(a) -> 7 % (a - 50); let (s1, s2) = object_for(<&!>) <@> (reduce(p, (+)), reduce(e, (+)))` with d = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 0, 9, 10] -- the compiled joint traversal fails at cell 1 (integer modulo by zero), the interpreter forces `p` (and through it `e`) whole and reports `e` at cell 7 (integer division by zero); a per-cell join evaluator (or a prefix probe of the leg over the share) is NOT built. The simpler sketches -- a leg operand (loops/669) or a leg fold kernel (loops/672) failing before the slot -- agree in every lane. Codex CLASS + diff review round 1: MERGE WITH CHANGES (3 members: the nested-map join -- open --, the inline operand order, the seed order -- fixed). Diff review round 2 MERGE WITH CHANGES (3): a FUSED TREE `reduce(p <&!> q, (+), 7 % z)` evaluated its seed before its legs named deferred operands (the C++ prelude forces them first: `let g = method_for(d) <@> lambda(a) -> 100 / a; let p = method_for(g) <@> lambda(a) -> a + 1; let q = method_for(d) <@> lambda(a) -> a + 2; let (s, t) = reduce(p <&!> q, (+), 7 % z)` with d = [1, 0, 2], z = 0: C++ integer division by zero) -- the interpreter now follows the C++ order, but the LLVM lane initializes the seed first and reads `g` fused per cell (integer modulo by zero): a third order, so the program is NOT pinned in the corpus (OPEN: the LLVM fused-tree fold should force its legs named deferred operands before the seeds, as the C++ prelude does); still OPEN: (i) a RUNTIME range operand (`method_for(0..(100 / z), mask(d, lambda(a) -> 200 % a > 0))`) evaluates its extent in operand order in C++ (division first), the interpreter keeps the range virtual (the mask modulo first); (ii) an inline ROW VIEW of a stored array folded with a failing seed (`reduce(d(j), (+), 7 % z)` with j = 5 out of range): C++ initializes the accumulator first (modulo), the interpreter evaluates the view first (BL8006). Found: an UNCAPTURED deferred key map / mask is a C++ BL9002 (gap G225). Before: **OPEN** | the programs above (for (3): loops/162 with a captured `g = 100 / a` map read in its kernel) | interp | Interp/Loops.fs: per-cell join evaluation; structural-source vs capture order; the halo guard after the capture prelude |

(1) and (2) STAGED, not built: (1) bind the target array once -- `{ let __arrN = (if c then a else b); __arrN(guarded k) = v }` -- in Zonk.zonkStmt / TExprAssign when needsBoundArray holds, sharing (never copying) the selected storage in every lane (the interpreter and the C++ wrapper share; the storage walk follows the if); (2) in Zonk.guardSubscriptsPlainAt's mixed arm, read a plain slot's RUN-TIME extent off the array at its RECORD position (not the subscript position: a compact group consumes several subscripts) -- programs `(if c then a else b)(k) = 99.0` with a runtime-extent `Idx<n>` and `let A: Array<Float64 like Idx<n>, SymIdx<2, 3>> = ..; A(i, 0, 1)` with runtime n and a computed i. ROUND 4 WAVE 9, lane C (C6; Codex design review 125dcb3f, verdict STAGE for the pair, (1) BUILD WITH CHANGES): NOT BUILT this wave (budget: C1-C5 first). Corrections to the sketch: (1) the bound target must keep the ASSIGNMENT ORDER -- the interpreter evaluates RHS, then the array, then the indices, then stores (Interp/Core.fs ~816 / ~1495), while the native LLVM lane evaluates the array and indices BEFORE the RHS (EmitLlvm ~3312 / ~3326, a lane disagreement of its own when both may fail) -- so the rewrite is `{ let __rhs = rhs; let __arrN = <array expr>; __arrN(guarded k) = __rhs }`, shared between Zonk TExprAssign (~1486) and TStmtAssign (~1723), the synthetic let IsMutable = false (C++ wrapper copy and the interpreter's let share the storage; the LLVM lane shares an if-selected / call-returned pool pointer, but copies a plain IRVar / IRParam initializer ~5432, so the rewrite applies only to non-variable receivers); re-evaluating `extents(<expr>)` instead is unsound even for a pure expression (state it reads may change during index evaluation); (2) a RECORD position is no extent accessor: `extents` lowers by record position while compact storage expands records into physical axes (CodeGenExprSupport ~1592; C++ `.extents[dim]`, the interpreter `ba.Extents[dim]`), so for `Array<Float64 like SymIdx<2, 3>, Idx<n>>` record 1 reads 3, not n; sparse / compound storage has a `trailing_stride` and no `.extents` (nested_array_types.hpp ~142 / ~178; the interpreter answers the cardinality for every dim) -- needs a logical-record -> physical-axis mapping and a representation-specific trailing accessor. FURTHER MEMBERS (unchecked today): a runtime-shaped FIELD receiver `s.f(k)` (needsBoundArray excludes TExprField, arrayExtentExpr does not support fields) for reads and writes; a mixed compact access through an `if` / a call (`needsBoundArray` excludes mixed flat compact and tuple-key accesses); a LITERAL beside a structured slot with an unknown plain extent (the mixed arm skips all literals, Zonk ~1009); compound / sparse element writes (the interpreter has no VCompound assignment arm; full-key sparse writes only). Witnesses needed: RHS / target / index failure order, backing-store mutation through the bound target, fields / projections / curried targets, runtime literals, compact groups before and after the plain slot; record native LLVM vs fallback. Witness numbers reserved: index-types/606-614 (unused). |

| G221 | **FIXED (round 4 wave 8 lane a, items A1-A4; the wave-7 STAGE fix sketch built member by member, then five Codex class-closure rounds, continuation d71e628b). THE PREDICATE: `Optimize.retentionAnalyzer` -- one boolean per callable over the shared WriteFacts (callees, callbacks, outer deferred lets, the extents types carry; least fixpoint): a whole assignment of a storage-bearing value to a non-local target, ANY element / field / projection store of one except a single-subscript store straight into a private fresh local, a retaining callee / callback / kernel reference, an unresolved head given an array, an unresolved function-typed name, and the head expression of a call (`handlers((keep(y) : One))(1.0)`); exposed as `callableMayRetainWith` / `retentionCheckerWith` / `scheduleMayRetainIn` (Optimize) and `callableMayRetain` / `retentionChecker` / `exprMayRetain` (codegen). THE CONSUMERS: (1) computeScopeEscapes seed 4 -- a scope statement that may retain pins every binding it names and its own let id (allocations are stamped with the statement owner: a retaining call in a let rec step block); (2) CONTENTS PROVENANCE -- a fresh form whose element or result type can hold storage (closures, structs or tuples holding arrays) is no propagation barrier and a return of one pins its refs (m7: `[wrap(y)]`, a FreshPool call of closures over its argument, a fold returning a Box); (3) scratch pool reuse -- a retaining position or a fresh form with storage-holding cells never leaves a donor unaliased (planPoolReuse takes the predicate); (4) staging-copy elision -- a retaining statement naming the source keeps the copy, and the reads close over every named callable captures (a closure built before the alias); (5) the owned move -- a materialized value that may retain T or whose cells can hold storage, and a FreshPool call result whose cells can hold storage, decline the move (renderOwnedMove keeps reading SuppressNames only: frame.Escapes also holds return pins, memfree/051); (6) the fresh-return facts -- a callable that captures the returned let demotes the producer to NotFresh when the let naming that callable may retain (`prep` exporting y through a nested `keep`; a pure local reader keeps the producer fresh and its caller free, both pinned by blade test optimize); (7) bindInlineGathers / bindInlineArrayCall and their statement twins keep the temporary when the consumer may retain (the ninth member: `keep(F((0, _)))`); releaseInlinePools treats a pinned pool as retained, not as a compiler error. WITNESSES memfree/074-099 (each pinned to the interpreter value; 074-080, 082-088, 091-099 reported AddressSanitizer heap-use-after-free or a wrong value at the base, all ASan-clean now; 081 (m6) is not a reproducing member in C++ -- the assignment materializes the map -- kept as a regression pin; 089 / 090 the non-retaining twins, blade test optimize pins the frees both ways); blade test owned-move pins 086; blade test optimize decision_pool_reuse_retaining_lambda_kernel_declined. LLVM (A5, recorded per member): it refuses 074, 076, 078, 081, 084-086, 091 (a function rebinding a whole module-level array), 077 (a tuple parameter), 079 / 088 / 092 (an array of functions), 080 / 090 / 098 / 099 (IRStructLit), 082 / 083 (an array-returning call of closures), 087 (a sparse initializer), 093 (a struct return), 094-097 (an array of structs) -- each a recorded refusal with the C++ fallback -- and runs 075 and 089 with the interpreter values (Codex: its G78 ownership handoff / G204 escape handling covers 075). Cost: retaining statements are rare; a retained pool is now owned by the retainer (a leak, never a use after free). REVIEWS (Codex, one continuation d71e628b): design BUILD WITH CHANGES (4 findings: classify at the invocation node, fresh roots do not prove destinations private, the inline-gather ninth member, lifetime regions must accept retention); diff rounds 2-5 found 3 + 2 + 2 + 1 more members (loop owners, call heads, aggregate fold results; contents provenance in the owned move and scratch reuse plus a regression of the emitter Escapes check; FreshPool call contents, closure readers of a staging source; a producer exporting its return through a capture), each reproduced under ASan and fixed with a witness; round 6 one leak regression (an unconditional demotion), fixed; round 7 MERGE (tuple / nested-closure / mut-parameter escapes checked, none unsafe).** **WAS STAGED (wave 7) -- NEXT-WAVE ITEM 1 (round 4 wave 7 lane c, item C7; the lead's rule: a fix not closable in one batch is written up, not half-built). Codex design review (continuation f2bfb9b3) verdict STAGE: a per-call seed `pin every array-typed IRVar argument of a call whose callee may retain an argument` (computeScopeEscapes) fixes the direct witness but does not close the class. THE CLASS = storage of the caller reachable through a value that OUTLIVES the scope that allocated it -- through views, aggregate members, closure environments and deferred operands -- and retention must block DESTRUCTIVE REUSE as well as the free. MEMBERS (each a program; C++ lane): (m1) a module mut assignment in a callee: `let mut saved: Array<Float64 like I> = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]; function keep(a: Array<Float64 like I>) -> Int64 = { saved = a; 3 }; function f(x: Array<Float64 like I>) -> Float64 = { let mut y = x * 2.0 |> compute; let n = keep(y); let z = x * 3.0 |> compute; reduce(z, (+)) + saved(0) + Float64(n) }; let x: Array<Float64 like I> = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0]; let result = f(x)` -- the C++ lane must print `result = 23.0` and `saved = [2.0, 4.0, 6.0]`; today ASan heap-use-after-free at saved's print; (m2) an enclosing let mut captured by a nested function: `function outer(x) = { let mut saved = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]; function keep(a) = { saved = a; Int64(3) }; function worker(a) = { let y = a * 2.0 |> compute; keep(y) }; let n = worker(x); saved(0) + Float64(n) }` -- must print `result = 5.0` for x = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0]; (m3) the argument is not a plain IRVar: an interior view `keep(y((0 : R)))`, a tuple `keep_pair((y, 0))` with `saved = t[0]`, or a row kernel `method_for(y) <@> keep |> compute` (IRLift.liftCallArg does not normalize every storage-bearing argument; kernel application passes arguments through IRApplyCombinator, not IRApp); (m4) a store through a LOCAL VIEW of caller storage: `function stash(out: mut Array<(Float64 -> Float64) like R, One>, a: Array<Float64 like I>) -> Int64 = { let slot = out((0 : R)); slot(0) = lambda(k: Float64) -> reduce(a, (+)) * k; 3 }` -- `slot` is let-bound in the callee but names out's storage: lexical locality is not storage provenance (Optimize write analysis, the rebinding rule); (m5) a struct field / external array cell: `struct Box { a: Array<Float64 like I> }`, `keep(a) = { box.a = a; 3 }`; (m6) a deferred producer stored externally: `keep(a) = { saved = method_for(a) <@> lambda(v) -> v * 2.0; 3 }`; (m7) a FRESH CONTAINER holding a closure over the argument: `function wrap(a) = lambda(k: Float64) -> reduce(a, (+)) * k; function build(x) = { let y = x * 2.0 |> compute; [wrap(y)] }; let fs = build(x); let result = fs(0)(1.0)` -- must print `result = 12.0`; IR.fs treats array literals / materialized applications as fresh barriers and computeScopeEscapes omits return seeds for array literals, so the outer pool's freshness hides the contents' provenance; (m8) DESTRUCTIVE REUSE of a retained pool (no free needed): staging-copy elision (`canAliasStagingLet`: `let y = x * 2.0 |> compute; let n = keep(y); let mut z = y; z(0) = 100.0` -- the elided copy writes into saved's storage; saved must keep [2.0, 4.0, 6.0]), scratch pool reuse (a donor retained through a retaining ROW KERNEL `method_for(y) <@> keep` -- `callsCallable` sees ordinary IRApp only) and the owned move (`insideFresh` does not follow a named kernel's retention, and `renderOwnedMove` checks SuppressNames, not `frame.Escapes`, so `y = z` frees a retained y). NOT members: a whole `mut` parameter rebound (BL4005 refuses it); provider writers and display.emit (they serialize their inputs synchronously: NetCDF, Zarr, CSV; Icechunk refuses writes); returned aliases / tuples / closures by direct return (the existing NotFresh return facts and backward propagation cover them: memfree/005, functions/331 -- keep regressions); copy in place (its module-mut admission already refuses alias-shaped occurrences, call arguments included); the early pool release (its admission takes closed scalar observations only); main's ledger frees nothing at exit (but its owned moves do: m8). LLVM: frees function-body pools on a scalar / unit return, but REFUSES the m1 module rebinding (EmitLlvm, the program runs in C++); capturing callables have their own lifetime handling, not repaired by a C++ seed. Lifetime regions (IRReleasePools): an independent release authority whose IRValidate later-reference check is not an interprocedural retention proof (the G69 boundary). FIX SKETCH (Codex, fail closed): (1) one RETENTION predicate `mayRetainArgs` over a callee, transitive through every callable it names (calls, kernels, captured closures; an unresolved callee = true), over its body and embeddedExtentExprs: a whole-array assignment to a non-local target, or ANY non-scalar indexed / field store whose destination is not PROVED private (follow aliases, views, projections, rebindings -- the write analysis's storage-provenance rule, never lexical locality); (2) seed the storage dependencies of the COMPLETE argument expressions (views, tuples, closure environments) of every invocation form -- IRApp, kernel operands of maps / folds / compositions -- whose callee may retain; (3) the same retention facts gate scratch reuse (donor), the owned move (renderOwnedMove must also honour frame.Escapes) and staging-copy elision; (4) a fresh aggregate does not stop propagation through its ELEMENTS (contents provenance distinct from the outer allocation). The scope-exit escape seeds pin a call argument only through captures and assignments the scope itself makes, not through a callee that retains the argument | pin (or copy) an array argument of any call whose callee may retain it (the write analysis already knows `keep` writes a module binding), or make a whole-array assignment of a parameter to a module binding a copy | the caller's local is freed while a module binding names it | pre-existing (master 62427ae2) | C++ | FIXED (wave 8 lane a) |

STAGED (pre-existing, same provenance hole): the kernel own `args[k]` over `range<Idx<arity(xs)>>` of ANOTHER pack is still unrolled by args arity. STAGED, option B: a select chain over a HOMOGENEOUS pack (`let j = e; check 0 <= j < n (BL8006 at the subscript); if j == 0 then p0 else ..`) in the specialization rewrite, which would compile arity/501 and 502 in every lane; needs the full-type homogeneity test, empty and tail-view packs and failure-order witnesses (Codex). **Option B STAGED (round 4 wave 9, lane d; Codex design review, continuation 60de99c8: STAGE).** The scalar select chain is the right primitive (fresh lets sequence the index, then an IRConstraintCheck BL8006 guard, then a lazy IRIf chain over p0..p{n-1}; C++ conditionals and LLVM branches are lazy), but option B as proposed needs pieces that do not exist: (1) ROLLED-KERNEL CLONING -- the ordinary call rewrite expands pack arguments only in the specialized body (IRMono `rewrite` over func.Body); a former kernel is a separately lifted callable (funcMap), so a rolled `pick(args, k)` needs a per-specialization kernel clone with ONE pack capture replaced by N element captures (rebindThroughClosures renames one-to-one) and its new call demands enqueued (the worklist scans spec.Body only); mutating the shared kernel would contaminate other arities; (2) RANGE PROVENANCE -- `Idx<arity(args)>` reaches IRMono as an opaque IRParam extent, so keeping the former rolled does not recover the iteration domain: `function first(p: Poly<Float64>) -> Float64 = p[0]; function f(xs: Poly<Float64>, args: Poly<Float64>) -> Float64 = { let terms = method_for(range<Idx<arity(xs)>>) <@> lambda(k) -> first(args) + Float64(k) |> compute; reduce(terms, (+)) }; let r = f((1.0, 2.0), (10.0, 20.0, 30.0))` must print 21.0 (not 33.0, not BL8006); (3) HOMOGENEITY -- arity specialization precedes HM and shape specialization and builds every element parameter with one baseType, so the full-type test (units, widths, component types, array shapes, nominal index identity) must come from the call-instance types or be deferred; unknown refuses; (4) LOCATION -- IRPolyIndex carries no span (Lowering ~744), so the BL8006 guard cannot report the subscript until one is kept; (5) an empty pack stays BL7004 at lowering. CLASS (every surviving use of an eliminated pack representation): direct / folded / ordinal reads (arity/502, 504, 507, 495), named forwarding (501, 505), captured pack and ordinal and transitive callable aliases (484, 485, 487, 489), pack aliases, projections, lambda arguments (488, 490-492, 506), a callable in a tuple or a match binder (494, 498), inline formers and arity-only dependence (496, 497, 499), offset / oversized / independent-pack ranges (508, 509, f above); new members: `function pick_div(p: Poly<Float64>, d: Int64) -> Float64 = p[1 / d]` (BL8013 at the division first), `function ordered(p: Poly<Float64>, j: Int64, d: Int64) -> Float64 = { let x = p[j]; x + Float64(1 / d) }; ordered(10.0, 20.0, 2, 0)` (BL8006 first), a dynamic tail-view read `let h :: t = p; t[j]` (stays BL7004). | G233 | found by round 4 wave 8 lane B (B1, Codex design review): the G214 rule (a kernel capture forces once under its own name) where the traversal cannot read the snapshot -- (a) a fold kernel capturing its own spine when the spine is not one rank-1 operand: `let m: Array<Float64 like Idx<2>, Idx<3>> = ..; let p = method_for(m) <@> lambda(v) -> v + 1.0; let s = reduce(p, lambda(a, b) -> a + b + Float64(extents(p)), 0.0)` (C++: backend limit BL7004 naming G233; interpreter: unsupported); (b) a captured LEAF of a fusion tree `reduce(p <&!> q, (+))` whose kernel captures p (C++ backend limit; the reduction join with tuple kernels keeps its per-cell share); (c) a FUNCTION-BODY kernel capturing a module deferred map is evaluated privately per call (G75, loops/578) -- by design, not snapshotted | a stored-leaf form of the fused fold in both compiled lanes and the interpreter | a refusal (no wrong value) | round 4 wave 8 lane B | C++, interpreter | **STAGED (round 4 wave 9, lane B, B3; Codex 305000ef round 3: BUILD WITH CHANGES, beyond the wave budget after B1/B2/B4).** (a) as written does not reproduce: `reduce(p, k, s)` over a rank-2 deferred p is the DEFAULT innermost partial fold (`s = [15.0, 24.0]`, C++ emits no #error); the full-fold member needs a fused-terminal witness first (`axes = 2`). (b) The identity-apply sketch is UNSOUND as written: replacing a captured leaf p by `method_for(p) <@> lambda(v) -> v` deletes p's own capture-prelude position -- `let x: Array<Int64 like Idx<2>> = [0, 1]; let e = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 10 / v; let d = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 7 % v; let p = method_for(e) <@> lambda(v) -> v + extents(d); let q = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> v + extents(p); let s = reduce(p <&!> q, (+))` fails in d (the modulo: p leaf capture first) as written but in e (the division) with the identity leaf. Members: that tree; `reduce(p <&!> q, lambda(a, b) -> a + b + p(0), 0.0)` (a fold-kernel capture of a leaf); the reduction-join entry point (inferReductionJoin builds its terminal directly: `object_for(<&!>) <@> (reduce(p, (+)), reduce(q, (+)))`); a different-rank tree (the LLVM equal-shape requirement); a closed-form domain leaf (`range<Band>`: a stored sparse operand loses the domain admission); a tuple-element leaf. FIX SKETCH: keep the captured leaf's NAME in the tree (the KeptSpineFolds mark per leaf, `IRFusion (IRVar p, ..)`), so every lane's prelude resolves the original leaf kernels in order, then fold a leaf that the prelude STORED through an identity traversal built AFTER the prelude (C++ genReduceComputeBindingCore / genReduceJoinCore and the join-share logic computed after the store; the interpreter multi-leaf path; the LLVM IRVar leg already exists), and apply the same normalization in inferReductionJoin. (c) A FUNCTION-body kernel capturing a module deferred map evaluated privately per call (G75, loops/578) is BY DESIGN; the C++ holder mechanism is lane C's (G239); the LLVM lane now evaluates such a capture privately per reading statement (G235 (b)). |
| G234 | found by round 4 wave 8 lane B (B2): an EXTENT read of a deferred map whose extent is known only at run time -- `let iv: Array<Int64 like Idx<4>> = [1, 0, 2, -1]; let c = compound(iv, mask(iv, lambda(v) -> v >= 0)); let d = method_for(c) <@> lambda(v) -> Float64(100 / v); let fz: Float64 = 0.0; let n = extents(d); let fail = Int64(floor(fz / fz))` -- the C++ lane and the interpreter force d (BL8013 at d: the extent renders `d.idx->cardinality` by name), the LLVM lane reads the operand cardinality (BL8014 at `fail`, the agreed outcome under the G218 rule) | the shape of a deferred map read from its operands (a one-operand elementwise map has its operand shape) in the C++ lane and the interpreter | a failure-site divergence (the LLVM lane is right) | round 4 wave 8 lane B | C++, interpreter | **FIXED (round 4 wave 9, lane B, B4).** One rewrite before the lane split, Lowering.resolveDeferredExtentsModule (after the unseeded-seed resolution, before the optimizer): in main-level code (module binding values; kernels are lifted callables, so a capture that reads only `extents(d)` inside a kernel still forces d, G214), `extents(d, k)` of a deferred ELEMENTWISE map (one operand or a zip co-iteration, cell kernel inputs and output, no Reynolds, an unsymmetric output of the operand's rank) whose extent at k is not static becomes `extents(o, k)` of its first operand o -- through deferred aliases and chains of such maps -- when o is a stored binding; anything else keeps the force (formalism 10.2 says so). The C++ lane and the interpreter now agree with the LLVM lane: BL8014 at `fail`. Witnesses loops/794 (the compound), 795 (a zip through an alias, in arithmetic), 796 (a chain of maps). A zip whose operands differ at run time reads the first operand (the never-evaluated map does not fail BL8011: the non-forcing rule; Codex agrees). STAGED members (the interpreter and C++ force, the LLVM lane reads the operand; Codex 305000ef round 4): a range with a run-time bound (`let k = extents(c); let d = method_for(0..k) <@> lambda(i) -> Float64(100 / i); let n = extents(d)`: typeOf of a range is its element type, the shape test does not admit it), a projection of a wholly deferred tuple (`let t = (d, d); let e = t[0]; let n = extents(e)`), a block-valued deferred map (`let d = { let z: Int64 = 0; method_for(c) <@> lambda(v) -> 100 / v + z }; let n = extents(d)`), a function-body extent read of a run-time-shaped module map, an inline-computation operand, a type-carried extent. TYPE-CARRIED member verified (wave-end fable review; the rewrite never visits it: an index type's extent is not an ExprShape child): `let iv: Array<Int64 like Idx<4>> = [1, 0, 2, -1]; let c = compound(iv, mask(iv, lambda(v) -> v >= 0)); let d = method_for(c) <@> lambda(v) -> Float64(100 / v); let fz: Float64 = 0.0; let r = reduce(0..extents(d), (+)); let fail = Int64(floor(fz / fz))` (and `let y = method_for(0..extents(d)) <@> lambda(i) -> i + 1 |> compute`) -- the interpreter BL8013 at d, the C++ lane a BL9002 (`d` not declared: the range bound's extent is rendered by name and nothing forced d; pre-existing -- the rewrite does not change this program's IR), the LLVM lane declines (a range over a run-time extent, C++ fallback). Not a one-line fix (the extent sits in the range's index type and in every type derived from it): STAGED. |
| G235 | found by round 4 wave 8 lane B (G214 Codex diff review round 2): two remaining members of the capture-forcing class, where the lanes AGREE today but the G214 snapshot rule is not met -- (a) a fold whose spine p is forced INDIRECTLY (a captured map q of p forces p as its input) and whose seed WRITES an input of p: `let mut x: Array<Float64 like Idx<3>> = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0]; let p = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> v * 2.0; let q = method_for(p) <@> lambda(v) -> v + 1.0; let s = reduce(p, lambda(a, b) -> a + b + Float64(extents(q)), { x(0) = 10.0; Float64(0) })` -- every lane stores p = [2, 4, 6] and then folds a FRESH traversal over the written x (s = 39.0; the snapshot rule says 21.0): the typechecker substitutes the producer into the fold (tryInferReduceCompute), so the fold has no name to read back; (b) a FUNCTION-BODY fold over a stored operand whose kernel captures a module deferred map: `let x: Array<Int64 like Idx<3>> = [1, 0, 3]; let d = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 10 / v; function f() -> Int64 = { reduce(x, lambda(a, b) -> a + b + extents(d), 0) }; let s = f()` -- after lane C's merge the C++ body fold forces the capture under a holder (BL8013, as the interpreter's private force); the remaining split is the LLVM lane, whose emitReduce capture prelude is skipped `if not c.InFunction` (prints 13) | (a) keep the named spine through the substitution and fold its snapshot after the capture forces; (b) a private per-call capture force on the function expression path in both compiled lanes | (a) a value the rule does not give (lanes agree); (b) a BL9002 and a dropped failure | round 4 wave 8 lane B | C++, LLVM, interpreter | **FIXED (round 4 wave 9, lane B, B1)** except the staged residual below. (a) The checker keeps the spine NAME when a capture of the fold-kernel LAMBDA forces it (TypedAst.markKeptSpine -- a TExprCompute wrapper with a reserved span on the fold node itself, so the decision survives zonking and two folds over one p never share it (wave-end fable review: a side table keyed on the spine occurrence's (binder, span) could confuse synthesized folds sharing one span; Codex 7184896d: a poisoned key would have sent a kept fold down the ordinary reduce, reversing the capture order) -- TypeCheckInfer.tryInferReduceCompute keepSpineName: a narrow closure over captures through callable aliases, value aliases, top-level projections of wholly deferred tuples, let-only block values, map operands and map kernel captures; module level and top-level blocks; a rank-1 scalar-element spine that IS the map binding, not an alias root; not a parallel fold kernel), and Lowering lowers exactly the marked occurrences to `IRReduceCompute (IRVar p, ..)`: every lane keeps its ordered capture prelude (leaf kernels, then fold kernels) and folds the stored spine (C++ spineCaptured / rootStored -> genReduceBinding; interpreter leavesAfter / storedRoot; LLVM the IRVar leg, the spine exclusion of the capture loop lifted for a named root). (b) The LLVM function path: forceCapture's function arm evaluates a capture of a deferred MODULE map privately per call (forcePrivatePrereqs: its deferred module inputs and captures first; then forceHoistable -> materializeExpr, else refusePrivateUnhoistable, C++ fallback); the capture loops of emitReduce, forcePrivateOperandInputs, applyToArr, forceCopyInputs and the private-map materialization run in function bodies; the private producer descriptors are installed under BuildingDeferred and RESTORED at every spine statement of the body (Ctx.PrivateDescriptors, positionalStatement), so a private force lives for its reading statement only, as the C++ re-force does (Codex diff review round 1: a pool kept across statements read stale cells after an input write -- also pre-existing for plain cell reads, loops/865). Witnesses loops/851-862, 865. | G236 | found by round 4 wave 8 lane B (G213, Codex review round 5): the LLVM CHOICE evaluates its operands lazily (emitArr IRChoice selects per cell, reading the right operand only in the else branch), while the C++ lane and the interpreter evaluate each operand WHOLE, left then right, before selecting. Remaining (pre-existing on cf43784a, Codex round 6) -- (a) an operand read only in the untaken branch: `let x: Array<Int64 like Idx<3>> = [1, 2, 3]; let q = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 10 / (v - 2); let c = x <|> q; let s = reduce(c, (+), 0)` and the inline `let c = (x <|> q) |> compute` (LLVM completes, s = 6; C++ and the interpreter BL8013); FIXED in this wave: (b) the operand ORDER: `let mut x: Array<Int64 like Idx<3>> = [1, 0, 3]; let p = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 10 / v; let d = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 20 % v; let q = method_for(d) <@> lambda(v) -> v + 1; let c = p <|> q; let s = reduce(c, (+), 0); x(2) = 4` -- FIXED ONLY IN A PROGRAM THAT ASSIGNS (the copy prerequisites are persisted only there): the left operand is evaluated privately before the right operand inputs are stored, for a choice AND a fallback (functions/676, 678 `<|:>`, fable review); WITHOUT an assignment the lazy per-cell choice still orders failures BY CELL -- `p = 10 / (v - 3)` failing at cell 2 beside `d = 20 % v` failing at cell 1 reports the modulo in the LLVM lane, the division in C++ and the interpreter (open, with (a)); (c) the round-4 discarded private copies of both operands were removed (one extra evaluation of the LEFT operand remains, only in an assigning program, to keep (b) ordered). Tried and reverted in this wave: materializing both operands in the choice emitter (left then right) closes (a)-(c) but makes every stale-producer REBUILD of a module choice emit code, so refreshProducers refuses the program (C++ fallback) -- loops/725 and functions/675, 677 lost the LLVM lane | the choice operands evaluated whole, in order, at the choice consumption, in a form a deferred rebuild can carry (an operand-copy node the producer refers to, materialized at the consuming statement) | (a)-(b) a dropped or reordered program-determined failure in the LLVM lane; (c) extra work | round 4 wave 8 lane B | LLVM | **STAGED** (Codex round 5; round 4 wave 9 lane B design review, Codex 8493d73b: STAGE). Target design: an explicit, descriptor-only PREPARATION dependency on the LLVM producer (`Choice (Copy left, Copy right)`: building it emits no code, so refreshProducers rebuilds it purely), realized at the CONSUMPTION in order -- left prerequisites, the whole left copy, then the right, then selection; the statement-prefix hoist of materializeExpr for a module choice's first forcing use, the evaluation point for an inline choice, per application inside a kernel, the frame for a function-local choice, per reading statement for a function reading a module choice (the G235 (b) descriptor reset) -- propagated through every traversal entry (materialize, folds and joins, prodsum, scalar-cell map operands that bypass materialize, fused reduction leaves, call arguments, returns, printing); the discarded left copy of the assigning-program workaround is replaced by the actual pool. `<|:>` stays declined in the LLVM lane, and its dense-left C++ / interpreter disagreement (C++ prepares both operands, Interp forceFallback only the left) needs its own parity witness. Class programs (with `let x: Array<Int64 like Idx<3>> = [1, 2, 3]; let q = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 10 / (v - 2)`): a named choice folded, an anonymous choice folded, `(x <|> q) |> compute`, a choice as a map operand, a nested choice, an alias, a tuple copy, a positional read `c(0)`, `prodsum(x <|> q, x)`, a reduction join, `object_for(<|>) <@> (x, q)`, a block-local choice, a choice inside a kernel and a function body; acceptance must ASSERT the LLVM route of loops/725 and functions/675, 677. |

| G238 | found by round 4 wave 8 lane C (G190 side bug (ii); lane C keeps its own row G238 in its block -- this row is lane B's record of the fix): a module deferred map read inside a TUPLE-RETURNING (or capturing) function body printed in the LLVM lane only -- `type A = Array<Float64 like Idx<2>>; let g: A = [2.0, 0.0]; let delayed = method_for(g) <@> lambda(v) -> v * 2.0; function h(x: Float64) -> (Float64, Float64) = (x * delayed(0), x); let r = h(Float64(1.0))` printed `delayed = [4.0, 0.0]` (every ad.grad derivative call: ad/084-087 printed `forcing = [2.0]`); and the inlined force was a SNAPSHOT: after `g(0) = 5.0` a second call read the first call cells (r2 = (4.0, 1.0), C++ and the interpreter 10.0) | the LLVM lane expands such a call in place in main (withCalleeDecl) but must keep the C++ call semantics: a function-body force is private per call (G75) | an extra printed line and a stale value in the LLVM lane | round 4 wave 8 lane C | LLVM | **FIXED (round 4 wave 8, lane B).** Ctx.InlinedCallee counts the open inlined bodies (withCalleeDecl); inside one, markForced and forceCapture make no main force (no print, no snapshot; the captures are still walked) and materializeExpr evaluates a module deferred map privately (no hoist, no slot upgrade). | G239 | found by round 4 wave 8 lane C (C1 design review, Codex round 1), NOT fixed (the G60 / G77 class): inside a FUNCTION body the C++ lane forces a deferred module map once per READING STATEMENT, before the statement (the statement pre-pass, now uniform: genFuncBodyScoped.moduleDeferredReadsOf), while the interpreter forces it at each READ. They differ when (1) a failure earlier in the same statement must come first -- `let a = (1 / z) + forcing(0)` with both failing (C++: the map's failure; interpreter: BL8013 of `1 / z`); (2) two reads of one statement straddle a write of the map's input -- a `mut` argument write between them (since G190 (c) refused when the mut argument shares the map's input, BL4005); (3) a read in a loop step after the loop wrote the input; (4) a read in an UNTAKEN if arm or a short-circuited && / || operand -- `let a = if x > 0.0 then forcing(0) else 1.0` with a failing map fails in C++ only (formalism ~2624 records it under G77). The evaluation COUNT differs too (C++ once per reading statement, the interpreter once per read): cost only, since a WRITING named map is stored at its definition (G45). Each pre-existing (the loop / return pre-passes already ran first); formalism 10.2 records it OPEN under G77 for callable bodies | per-read evaluation inside callable bodies, or a refusal where the order is observable | a failure-order divergence | lane C wave 8 | C++ (LLVM hoists the same way) | CodeGen.genFuncBodyScoped (moduleDeferredReadsOf / emitBodyLet) | **OPEN -- STAGED by round 4 wave 9 lane C (C1; Codex design review e6f9cd20, verdict STAGE)**. CONTRACT CONFIRMED (no semantics question): formalism 10.2 places a pure deferred map's evaluation at its FORCING USE; the "start of the body statement" wording (~2505) describes the compiled lanes and the order inside callable bodies is recorded OPEN there -- inside a callable body every ORDINARY forcing occurrence (a positional / extent / membership read, a whole-array argument, a prodsum operand) evaluates the map AT ITS POSITION in evaluation order, an untaken read (an if / match arm, a short-circuited && / || operand, a while guard's right operand) evaluates nothing, and a kernel CAPTURE is one snapshot at its traversal's prelude (after the traversal's named inputs, before its first cell, also for zero cells -- G115). THE COUNT IS OBSERVABLE, not cost only (the G45 argument covers a map that WRITES, not a write to the map's INPUT): measured at e1fbc7a5 with `type A = Array<Float64 like Idx<1>>; let mut backing: A = [1.0]; let forcing = method_for(backing) <@> lambda(e) -> e * 2.0` -- (p3) `function f(x: Float64) -> Float64 = { let rec s: Array<Float64 like Idx<4>> = match s with | zero -> zero | zero :: k -> zero :: forcing(0) | prefix :: n -> prefix :: { backing(0) = Float64(n); forcing(0) }; reduce(s, (+)) }; let r = f(5.0)`: interpreter 14.0 (a force per read per step), C++ and LLVM 8.0; (p5) `function bump(x: Float64) -> Float64 = { backing(0) = x; 0.0 }; function f(x: Float64) -> Float64 = { let a = forcing(0) + bump(x) + forcing(0); a }`: interpreter 12.0, C++ / LLVM 4.0; (p2) `let a = forcing(0) + { backing(0) = x; forcing(0) }` in a body: interpreter and C++ 20.0 (IRLift moves the block's write ahead of both reads), LLVM 4.0 (it forces at the start of the whole lowered statement, BEFORE the lifted write -- an LLVM-only member). Order members, with `let mut backing: A = [-1.0]; let forcing = method_for(backing) <@> lambda(e) -> Int64(floor(sqrt(e)))`: (p1) `function f(z: Int64) -> Int64 = { let a = (1 / z) + forcing(0); a }; let r = f(0)`: interpreter BL8013 (line of `1 / z`), C++ and LLVM BL8014 (the map); the RETURN twin `function f(z: Int64) -> Int64 = (1 / z) + forcing(0)`; (p4) `let a = if x > 0.0 then forcing(0) else 1` with x = -2.0: interpreter r = 1, C++ / LLVM BL8014; the `&&` twin `x > 0.0 && forcing(0) > 0` (interpreter false) and the `||` twin `x < 0.0 || forcing(0) > 0`; a MATCH arm `match z with | 0 -> 1 | _ -> forcing(0)` with z = 0 (collectDeferredPositionalReads visits every arm and guard); a WHILE GUARD `| prefix :: n while x > 0.0 && forcing(0) > 0 -> prefix :: prefix(n - 1)` with x = -1.0; an APPLIED LOCAL LAMBDA `{ let k = lambda(v: Int64) -> forcing(0) + v; let a = (1 / z) + k(1); a }` (the capture must not leap ahead of the division; binding an unused k stays inert, G241); a FOLD KERNEL capture in expression position `(1 / z) + reduce(v, lambda(a, b) -> a + b + forcing(0), 0)` (the capture belongs at the nested fold's prelude; the LLVM function capture prelude skips, EmitLlvm ~8829); the scalar if-return armIife path `function f() -> Float64 = if forcing(0) > 0 then sqrt(reduce(backing * backing, (+))) else 0.0` (CodeGen ~1314 renders the condition directly: audit it separately from the return pre-pass); an INLINED callee `function h(z: Int64) -> (Int64, Int64) = ((1 / z) + forcing(0), 0)` called from main and from a destructuring function (the LLVM main-level inline bypasses the hoist, EmitLlvm ~7404, an outlined call does not); the AD-generated bodies (Grad.fs ~598 assembles ordinary declarations: ad/084-087 must keep passing; first-failure candidate `function f(x: Float64, w: A) -> Float64 = { let y = w(Int64(floor(x))) + Float64(forcing(0)); y }` under `ad.grad(f)(Float64(5.0), w0, dw)`: the checked read before the map, AD admission unverified). A CAPTURE-ORDER member: with `let other = method_for(backing) <@> lambda(e) -> 1 / Int64(floor(e + 1.0))`, `function f() -> Int64 = { let ys = method_for(other) <@> lambda(v) -> v + forcing(0) |> compute; reduce(ys, (+), 0) }` must fail BL8013 (the named input `other`) before the capture's BL8014 -- the statement pre-pass pulls the capture ahead of the input (CodeGenBinding ~2104, Interp/Loops.fs ~1062). AN INTERPRETER MEMBER: the private force (Loops.fs ~299, unmemoized) and the capture prelude (~406) discard the forced value and resolveBinaryFold (~254) forwards the original cells, so a private capture is no retained snapshot. NEGATIVE CONTROL (must keep failing): a traversal whose kernel captures `forcing` but reads it only in an untaken cell branch still forces it before the first cell (G115), even over zero cells. WHY STAGED, NOT BUILT: (a) a lazy getter in place of the name (`H__get()[0]`) does NOT order anything -- `(1 / z) + H__get()[0]` is two unsequenced C++ operands (CodeGenExpr ~222 IRBinOp); the fix needs EXPLICIT ORDERED TEMPORARIES / expression-local statement blocks for the observable operands, branch-local forces for if / match / && / ||, kept apart from the lowering-order boundary IRLift already fixes (IRLift ~1090, which is why p2 gives 20 in the interpreter: keep it); (b) the existing materialization (forceDeferredArrayInput, recursive, dropping its dependencies from the deferred context) cannot move unchanged into a conditional scope: the frame frees name the inner wrappers and owned extents (CodeGenLoopNest renderFrameFrees), so ownership transfer of the holder, its dependencies and its run-time extents table must be designed, and a conditional force must not mark its dependencies materialized for other branches; (c) an eager statement-prefix force may stay only under a certificate over the WHOLE producer (IRTotality.auditApply audits a kernel, not a failing deferred input) and its input stability, ordered over every intervening barrier (after m1 is forced its checked subscript can fail before m2), by binder identity with alias classes and deferred tuple members. FIX SKETCH: (1) an ordered FORCE-EVENT representation over the executable IR (ordinary force, capture snapshot, copy, fused consumption); (2) C++: emit the sequencing explicitly (operands with a force or a may-fail event bound to ordered locals in evaluation order, forces inside the branch that reads them, holders owning their result and metadata), the prefix force kept only with the certificate of (c); captures at the traversal prelude in its operand / capture order; (3) LLVM: an explicit recorded refusal (C++ fallback) wherever the certificate fails, across outlined functions, inlined callees, kernels and return paths (forceHoistable alone misses the special paths); (4) the COUNT: a shared lowering refusal (BL4005) of a callable-body statement in which a write that may reach a transitive input of a deferred module map (an assignment whose storage is a module binder the map reads, a callee / kernel writing one, mutable scalar captures included, an unresolved callee fail closed; loop back-edges counted) can occur after one forcing use and before another -- a read of an already captured snapshot is no new force -- OR fresh evaluation per forcing use with distinct owned results kept alive through their consumers; (5) the interpreter: bind a private capture's forced value in a traversal-local environment (no module memo). Witness numbers reserved for the fix: functions/764-766. G256 (lane B's row, a member at STATEMENT granularity, reported by the lead) FIXED this wave in the C++ lane (CodeGen.moduleDeferredReadsOf; Codex diff review 5f64f352 rounds 3-4): a deferred map that is an OPERAND of a MATERIALIZED map or an applied composition -- the statement's whole value -- is forced for THAT statement (fresh holder, before the kernel captures: a map's own order), so a later statement no longer reads the holder an earlier one filled: `let mut x: Array<Int64 like Idx<3>> = [1, 2, 3]; let d = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 10 / v; function f() -> Int64 = { let z = d(1); x(0) = 10; let a = method_for(d) <@> lambda(v) -> v + 1; a(0) }` gives s = 2 as the interpreter does (it was 11; functions/760), the no-earlier-read twin compiles (it was an undeclared name, BL9002; 761), the unfused-pipeline twin `pipeline <@> d` gives s = 3 (763); the LLVM side is lane B's G235 (b). NOT covered, a pre-existing FOLD member measured this wave: `type A = Array<Int64 like Idx<3>>; let mut x: A = [0, 1, 2]; let d = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 10 / v; let c = method_for(x) <@> lambda(v) -> 20 % v; function f() -> Int64 = { let s = reduce(method_for(d) <@> lambda(v) -> v + c(0), (+), 0); s }` -- the interpreter fails `integer division by zero` (d, the operand, first), the C++ pre-pass forces the map kernel's capture c first (`integer modulo by zero`): the fold over a map of d is left to its own prelude by design, so its operand / capture order is this row's (no witness: the lanes disagree). |
